Bible Quiz Questions – Old Testament – Volume 6: Major Prophets I
Isaiah–Jeremiah · 118 chapters · 944 questions
Collection index and Volume 1
Each chapter has eight questions: three Easy, three Medium and two Hard. The question order and multiple-choice answer positions are mixed. Difficulty is editorial: Hard questions require closer recall, comparison of passages or careful interpretation.
Quotation questions marked WEB use the World English Bible. Other questions preserve the study material’s wording or paraphrase a passage. Accept a clearly equivalent answer when exact wording is not being tested.
Read the passage in context before applying it today or connecting it to Christ. A character’s statement is not automatically the narrator’s conclusion. Theological questions use the course summaries listed below alongside Scripture; disputed readings are identified in the explanations.
Sources: Old testament course summary · Gospel Worldview course summary · World English Bible
Isaiah
Isaiah 1
Q1. Fill in the blank: In this paraphrase, the LORD said, Though your sins are like scarlet, they shall be as ______ as snow. (1:18) Easy
Answer: white. Isaiah 1:18 records: “"Come now, and let us reason together," says Yahweh: "Though your sins be as scarlet, they shall be as white as snow. Though they be red like crimson, they shall be as wool.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “The daughter of ______ is left like a shelter in a vineyard, Like a ______ in a field of melons, Like a besieged city.” (1:8, WEB) Medium
Answer: Zion; hut. The wording is “The daughter of Zion is left like a shelter in a vineyard, Like a hut in a field of melons, Like a besieged city.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… rewards. They don't judge the fatherless, Neither does the cause of the ______ come to them.” (1:23); “I will restore your judges as at the ______, And your counselors as at the beginning. Afterward you shall be called 'The city of righteousness, A …” (1:26, WEB) Hard
Answer: widow; first. The first passage reads “… rewards. They don't judge the fatherless, Neither does the cause of the widow come to them.”; the second reads “I will restore your judges as at the first, And your counselors as at the beginning. Afterward you shall be called 'The city of righteousness, A …”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… make many prayers, I will not hear. Your hands are full of arms.” (1:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… make many prayers, I will not hear. Your hands are full of blood.” It says blood, not arms.
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “The ox knows his owner, And the donkey his master's crib; But ______ doesn't know, My people don't consider.” (1:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Israel. The cited words are “The ox knows his owner, And the donkey his master's crib; But Israel doesn't know, My people don't consider.”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… and open sores. They haven't been closed, neither bandaged, neither soothed with ______.”? (1:6, WEB) Easy
A) oil B) pomegranates C) grapes D) bread
Answer: A. oil. Isaiah 1:6 uses oil: “… and open sores. They haven't been closed, neither bandaged, neither soothed with oil.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… sacrifices to me?," says Yahweh. "I have had enough of the burnt ______ of rams, And the fat of fed animals. I don't delight in the …”? (1:11, WEB) Medium
A) death B) thanksgiving C) salvation D) offerings
Answer: D. offerings. The passage specifies offerings: “… sacrifices to me?," says Yahweh. "I have had enough of the burnt offerings of rams, And the fat of fed animals. I don't delight in the …”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “Learn to do well. Seek justice, Relieve the oppressed, Judge the ______, Plead for the widow."” (1:17); “How the faithful city has become a prostitute! She was full of ______; righteousness lodged in her, But now murderers.” (1:21, WEB) Hard
A) melons; justice B) fatherless; honor C) fatherless; justice D) justice; fatherless
Answer: C. fatherless; justice. Verse 17 reads “Learn to do well. Seek justice, Relieve the oppressed, Judge the fatherless, Plead for the widow."”; verse 21 reads “How the faithful city has become a prostitute! She was full of justice; righteousness lodged in her, But now murderers.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Isaiah 2
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “Their ______ also is full of idols. They worship the work of their own hands, That which their own fingers have made.”? (2:8, WEB) Medium
A) vineyards B) countries C) land D) earth
Answer: C. land. The passage specifies land: “Their land also is full of idols. They worship the work of their own hands, That which their own fingers have made.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… of the earth, From before the terror of Yahweh, And from the ______ of his majesty, When he arises to shake the earth mightily.” (2:19); “… been made for themselves to worship, To the moles and to the ______;” (2:20, WEB) Hard
Answer: glory; bats. The first passage reads “… of the earth, From before the terror of Yahweh, And from the glory of his majesty, When he arises to shake the earth mightily.”; the second reads “… been made for themselves to worship, To the moles and to the bats;”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… And their spears into pruning-hooks. Nation shall not lift up sword against ______, Neither shall they learn war any more.”? (2:4, WEB) Easy
A) nation B) oil C) refuge D) divination
Answer: A. nation. Isaiah 2:4 uses nation: “… And their spears into pruning-hooks. Nation shall not lift up sword against nation, Neither shall they learn war any more.”
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “The lofty looks of man will be brought low, The haughtiness of men will be bowed down, And Yahweh alone will be exalted in that day.” (2:11, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “The lofty looks of man will be brought low, The haughtiness of men will be bowed down, And Yahweh alone will be exalted in that day.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… to the mountain of Yahweh, To the house of the God of ______; And he will teach us of his ways, And we will walk in …” (2:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Jacob. The cited words are “… to the mountain of Yahweh, To the house of the God of Jacob; And he will teach us of his ways, And we will walk in …”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “For all the cedars of ______, that are high and lifted up, For all the oaks of Bashan,” (2:13); “… shall be bowed down, And the haughtiness of men shall be brought ______; And Yahweh alone shall be exalted in that day.” (2:17, WEB) Hard
A) low; Lebanon B) Lebanon; glory C) Lebanon; low D) divination; low
Answer: C. Lebanon; low. Verse 13 reads “For all the cedars of Lebanon, that are high and lifted up, For all the oaks of Bashan,”; verse 17 reads “… shall be bowed down, And the haughtiness of men shall be brought low; And Yahweh alone shall be exalted in that day.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Their land is full of ______ and ______, Neither is there any end of their treasures. Their land also is full of horses, Neither is there …” (2:7, WEB) Medium
Answer: silver; gold. The wording is “Their land is full of silver and gold, Neither is there any end of their treasures. Their land also is full of horses, Neither is there …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q8. True or false: In the last days the mountain of the LORD's house shall be established. (2:2) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 2:2 records: “It shall happen in the latter days, that the mountain of Yahweh's house shall be established on the top of the mountains, And shall be raised above the hills; And all nations shall flow to it.”
Isaiah 3
Q1. Fill in the blank: In this paraphrase, the LORD will take away the finery of the daughters of ______. (3:16-18) Easy
Answer: Zion. Isaiah 3:16 records: “Moreover Yahweh said, "Because the daughters of Zion are haughty, And walk with outstretched necks and flirting eyes, Walking to trip as they go, Jingling ornaments on their feet;”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “The captain of ______, The honorable man, The counselor, The skilled craftsman, And the clever enchanter.” (3:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: fifty. The cited words are “The captain of fifty, The honorable man, The counselor, The skilled craftsman, And the clever enchanter.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “the signet rings, the ______ rings,” (3:21); “the hand-mirrors, the ______ linen garments, the tiaras, and the shawls.” (3:23, WEB) Hard
Answer: nose; fine. The first passage reads “the signet rings, the nose rings,”; the second reads “the hand-mirrors, the fine linen garments, the tiaras, and the shawls.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… are their oppressors, And women rule over them. My people, those who spices you cause you to err, And destroy the way of your paths.” (3:12, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… are their oppressors, And women rule over them. My people, those who lead you cause you to err, And destroy the way of your paths.” It says lead, not spices.
Q5. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “Yahweh will enter into ______ with the elders of his people, And their leaders: "It is you who have eaten up the vineyard. The spoil of …” (3:14); “the headdresses, the ankle chains, the sashes, the perfume ______, the charms,” (3:20, WEB) Hard
A) judgment; purses B) instruction; bottles C) bottles; judgment D) judgment; bottles
Answer: D. judgment; bottles. Verse 14 reads “Yahweh will enter into judgment with the elders of his people, And their leaders: "It is you who have eaten up the vineyard. The spoil of …”; verse 20 reads “the headdresses, the ankle chains, the sashes, the perfume bottles, the charms,”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… is ruined, and ______ is fallen; Because their tongue and their doings are against Yahweh, To provoke the eyes of his ______.” (3:8, WEB) Medium
Answer: Judah; glory. The wording is “… is ruined, and Judah is fallen; Because their tongue and their doings are against Yahweh, To provoke the eyes of his glory.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “The look of their faces testify against them. They parade their ______ like Sodom. They don't hide it. Woe to their soul! For they have brought …”? (3:9, WEB) Medium
A) rebellion B) deceit C) transgression D) sin
Answer: D. sin. The passage specifies sin: “The look of their faces testify against them. They parade their sin like Sodom. They don't hide it. Woe to their soul! For they have brought …”
Q8. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… by another, And everyone by his neighbor. The child will behave himself ______ against the old man, And the base against the honorable.”? (3:5, WEB) Easy
A) parade B) mirrors C) proudly D) bald
Answer: C. proudly. Isaiah 3:5 uses proudly: “… by another, And everyone by his neighbor. The child will behave himself proudly against the old man, And the base against the honorable.”
Isaiah 4
Q1. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… for a shade in the daytime from the heat, and for a life and for a shelter from storm and from rain.” (4:6, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… for a shade in the daytime from the heat, and for a refuge and for a shelter from storm and from rain.” It says refuge, not life.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “Yahweh will create over the whole ______ of Mount Zion, and over her assemblies, a cloud and smoke by day, and the shining of a flaming …” (4:5); “There will be a ______ for a shade in the daytime from the heat, and for a refuge and for a shelter from storm and from …” (4:6, WEB) Hard
Answer: habitation; pavilion. The first passage reads “Yahweh will create over the whole habitation of Mount Zion, and over her assemblies, a cloud and smoke by day, and the shining of a flaming …”; the second reads “There will be a pavilion for a shade in the daytime from the heat, and for a refuge and for a shelter from storm and from …”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… daughters of ______, and shall have purged the blood of Jerusalem from its midst, by the spirit of justice, and by the ______ of burning.” (4:4, WEB) Medium
Answer: Zion; spirit. The wording is “… daughters of Zion, and shall have purged the blood of Jerusalem from its midst, by the spirit of justice, and by the spirit of burning.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “______ women shall take hold of one man in that day, saying, "We will eat our own bread, and wear our own clothing: only let us …” (4:1, WEB) Easy
Answer: Seven. The cited words are “Seven women shall take hold of one man in that day, saying, "We will eat our own bread, and wear our own clothing: only let us …”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… a cloud and smoke by day, and the shining of a flaming ______ by night; for over all the glory will be a canopy.” (4:5); “There will be a pavilion for a ______ in the daytime from the heat, and for a refuge and for a shelter from storm and from …” (4:6, WEB) Hard
A) shade; fire B) fire; shade C) hold; shade D) fire; pavilion
Answer: B. fire; shade. Verse 5 reads “… a cloud and smoke by day, and the shining of a flaming fire by night; for over all the glory will be a canopy.”; verse 6 reads “There will be a pavilion for a shade in the daytime from the heat, and for a refuge and for a shelter from storm and from …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… that he who is left in Zion, and he who remains in ______, shall be called holy, even everyone who is written among the living in …”? (4:3, WEB) Easy
A) Hebron B) Judah C) Jerusalem D) Gilgal
Answer: C. Jerusalem. Isaiah 4:3 uses Jerusalem: “… that he who is left in Zion, and he who remains in Jerusalem, shall be called holy, even everyone who is written among the living in …”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… the shining of a flaming fire by night; for over all the ______ will be a canopy.”? (4:5, WEB) Medium
A) love B) wrath C) glory D) kindness
Answer: C. glory. The passage specifies glory: “… the shining of a flaming fire by night; for over all the glory will be a canopy.”
Q8. True or false: The Branch of the LORD will be beautiful and glorious. (4:2) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 4:2 records: “In that day, Yahweh's branch will be beautiful and glorious, and the fruit of the land will be the beauty and glory of the survivors of Israel.”
Isaiah 5
Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… And also cut out a winepress therein. He looked for it to ______ grapes, But it yielded wild grapes.” (5:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: yield. The cited words are “… And also cut out a winepress therein. He looked for it to yield grapes, But it yielded wild grapes.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… their blossom shall go up as dust; Because they have rejected the ______ of Yahweh of Hosts, And despised the word of the Holy One of …” (5:24); “None shall be weary nor stumble among them; None shall ______ nor sleep; Neither shall the belt of their loins be untied, Nor the latchet of …” (5:27, WEB) Hard
Answer: law; slumber. The first passage reads “… their blossom shall go up as dust; Because they have rejected the law of Yahweh of Hosts, And despised the word of the Holy One of …”; the second reads “None shall be weary nor stumble among them; None shall slumber nor sleep; Neither shall the belt of their loins be untied, Nor the latchet of …”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “Woe to those who draw ______ with cords of falsehood, And wickedness as with cart rope;” (5:18); “… those who call evil good, and good evil; Who put darkness for ______, And light for darkness; Who put bitter for sweet, And sweet for bitter!” (5:20, WEB) Hard
A) idolatry; light B) light; iniquity C) iniquity; Gathered D) iniquity; light
Answer: D. iniquity; light. Verse 18 reads “Woe to those who draw iniquity with cords of falsehood, And wickedness as with cart rope;”; verse 20 reads “… those who call evil good, and good evil; Who put darkness for light, And light for darkness; Who put bitter for sweet, And sweet for bitter!”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q4. Fill in the blank: In this paraphrase, the vineyard of the LORD of hosts is the house of ______. (5:7) Easy
Answer: Israel. Isaiah 5:7 records: “For the vineyard of Yahweh of Hosts is the house of Israel, And the men of Judah his pleasant plant: And he looked for justice, but, behold, oppression; For righteousness, but, behold, a cry of distress.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… But it will grow briers and thorns. I will also command the ______ that they rain no rain on it."”? (5:6, WEB) Easy
A) shoes B) clouds C) ears D) wickedness
Answer: B. clouds. Isaiah 5:6 uses clouds: “… But it will grow briers and thorns. I will also command the clouds that they rain no rain on it."”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “The harp, lyre, tambourine, and flute, with ______, are at their feasts; But they don't regard the work of Yahweh, Neither have they considered the operation …”? (5:12, WEB) Medium
A) wine B) barley C) milk D) pomegranates
Answer: A. wine. The passage specifies wine: “The harp, lyre, tambourine, and flute, with wine, are at their feasts; But they don't regard the work of Yahweh, Neither have they considered the operation …”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… to those who join house to house, Who lay ______ to field, until there is no ______, And you are made to dwell alone in the …” (5:8, WEB) Medium
Answer: field; room. The wording is “… to those who join house to house, Who lay field to field, until there is no room, And you are made to dwell alone in the …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… has enlarged its desire, And opened its mouth without measure; And their glory, their multitude, their pomp, and he who rejoices among them, descend into it.” (5:14, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… has enlarged its desire, And opened its mouth without measure; And their glory, their multitude, their pomp, and he who rejoices among them, descend into it.”
Isaiah 6
Q1. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… "Until cities are waste without inhabitant, And houses without man, And the ______ becomes utterly waste,” (6:11); “If there are yet a ______ in it, It also shall in turn be eaten up: As a terebinth, and as an oak, whose stock remains, …” (6:13, WEB) Hard
Answer: land; tenth. The first passage reads “… "Until cities are waste without inhabitant, And houses without man, And the land becomes utterly waste,”; the second reads “If there are yet a tenth in it, It also shall in turn be eaten up: As a terebinth, and as an oak, whose stock remains, …”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q2. True or false: Isaiah saw the Lord seated on a throne, high and lifted up. (6:1) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 6:1 records: “In the year that king Uzziah died, I saw the Lord sitting on a throne, high and lifted up; and his train filled the temple.”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… holy, is Yahweh of Hosts! The whole earth is full of his ______!"”? (6:3, WEB) Easy
A) justice B) righteousness C) joy D) glory
Answer: D. glory. Isaiah 6:3 uses glory: “… holy, is Yahweh of Hosts! The whole earth is full of his glory!"”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… For I am ______, because I am a man of unclean lips, and I dwell in the midst of a people of ______ lips: for my …” (6:5, WEB) Medium
Answer: undone; unclean. The wording is “… For I am undone, because I am a man of unclean lips, and I dwell in the midst of a people of unclean lips: for my …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… with it, and said, "Behold, this has touched your lips; and your iniquity is taken away, and your sin forgiven."” (6:7, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… with it, and said, "Behold, this has touched your lips; and your iniquity is taken away, and your sin forgiven."”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… his hand, which he had taken with the tongs from off the ______.”? (6:6, WEB) Medium
A) throne B) house C) altar D) ark
Answer: C. altar. The passage specifies altar: “… his hand, which he had taken with the tongs from off the altar.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… with their eyes, And hear with their ears, And understand with their ______, And turn again, and be healed."” (6:10); “He said, "Go, and tell this people, 'You hear indeed, But don't ______; And you see indeed, But don't perceive.'” (6:9, WEB) Hard
A) head; understand B) heart; understand C) heart; wings D) understand; heart
Answer: B. heart; understand. Verse 10 reads “… with their eyes, And hear with their ears, And understand with their heart, And turn again, and be healed."”; verse 9 reads “He said, "Go, and tell this people, 'You hear indeed, But don't understand; And you see indeed, But don't perceive.'”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Above him stood the seraphim. Each one had ______ wings. With two he covered his face. With two he covered his feet. With two he flew.” (6:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: six. The cited words are “Above him stood the seraphim. Each one had six wings. With two he covered his face. With two he covered his feet. With two he flew.”
Isaiah 7
Q1. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “He said, "Listen now, house of David: Is it not enough for you to try the patience of men, that you will try the patience of …” (7:13, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “He said, "Listen now, house of David: Is it not enough for you to try the patience of men, that you will try the patience of …”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… careful, and keep calm. Don't be afraid, neither let your heart be ______ because of these two tails of smoking firebrands, for the fierce anger of …”? (7:4, WEB) Easy
A) anger B) faint C) feet D) butter
Answer: B. faint. Isaiah 7:4 uses faint: “… careful, and keep calm. Don't be afraid, neither let your heart be faint because of these two tails of smoking firebrands, for the fierce anger of …”
Q3. Fill in the blank: In Isaiah's sign to Ahaz, the child is to be called ______, meaning God with us. (7:14) Easy
Answer: Immanuel. The sign belongs first to the crisis facing Ahaz. Matthew 1:22-23 applies the passage to Jesus; translations and interpreters discuss the Hebrew term rendered young woman or virgin.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… David, saying, "Syria is allied with Ephraim." His heart trembled, and the ______ of his people, as the trees of the forest tremble with the wind.” (7:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: heart. The cited words are “… David, saying, "Syria is allied with Ephraim." His heart trembled, and the heart of his people, as the trees of the forest tremble with the wind.”
Q5. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… the head of Syria is Damascus, and the head of ______ is Rezin; and within sixty-______ years Ephraim shall be broken in pieces, so that it …” (7:8, WEB) Medium
Answer: Damascus; five. The wording is “… the head of Syria is Damascus, and the head of Damascus is Rezin; and within sixty-five years Ephraim shall be broken in pieces, so that it …” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “and the head of Ephraim is Samaria, and the head of ______ is Remaliah's son. If you will not believe, surely you shall not be established.'"”? (7:9, WEB) Medium
A) Moab B) Bethel C) Samaria D) Jordan
Answer: C. Samaria. The passage specifies Samaria: “and the head of Ephraim is Samaria, and the head of Samaria is Remaliah's son. If you will not believe, surely you shall not be established.'"”
Q7. Multiple choice: What does reading the sign in both Isaiah and Matthew require? (7:10-17; Matthew 1:22-23) Hard
A) Attend to the sign's setting with Ahaz and to Matthew's application to Jesus. B) Deny that Matthew uses the passage. C) Assume every interpreter agrees on every detail. D) Ignore Ahaz and the surrounding crisis entirely.
Answer: A. Attend to the sign's setting with Ahaz and to Matthew's application to Jesus.. The course asks readers to hear the original context before making Christian connections. The Hebrew word and the sign's immediate referent are debated; Matthew explicitly connects the passage with Jesus' birth.
Q8. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… to refuse the evil, and choose the good, the land whose two ______ you abhor shall be forsaken.” (7:16); “… from the day that Ephraim departed from Judah; even the king of ______.” (7:17, WEB) Hard
A) kings; Nineveh B) brothers; Assyria C) kings; Assyria D) Assyria; kings
Answer: C. kings; Assyria. Verse 16 reads “… to refuse the evil, and choose the good, the land whose two kings you abhor shall be forsaken.”; verse 17 reads “… from the day that Ephraim departed from Judah; even the king of Assyria.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Isaiah 8
Q1. Multiple choice: Which word completes “Because this people have refused the ______ of Shiloah that go softly, and rejoice in Rezin and Remaliah's son;”? (8:6, WEB) Easy
A) countries B) waters C) wizards D) wings
Answer: B. waters. Isaiah 8:6 uses waters: “Because this people have refused the waters of Shiloah that go softly, and rejoice in Rezin and Remaliah's son;”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… Israel, for a gin and for a snare to the inhabitants of ______.” (8:14); “Many shall stumble thereon, and fall, and be broken, and be ______, and be taken.” (8:15, WEB) Hard
A) Sinai; snared B) Jerusalem; snared C) Jerusalem; distressed D) snared; Jerusalem
Answer: B. Jerusalem; snared. Verse 14 reads “… Israel, for a gin and for a snare to the inhabitants of Jerusalem.”; verse 15 reads “Many shall stumble thereon, and fall, and be broken, and be snared, and be taken.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “For before the child shall have ______ to cry, My father, and, My mother, the riches of Damascus and the spoil of Samaria shall be carried …” (8:4, WEB) Easy
Answer: knowledge. The cited words are “For before the child shall have knowledge to cry, My father, and, My mother, the riches of Damascus and the spoil of Samaria shall be carried …”
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… conspiracy!" concerning all about which this people shall say, "A conspiracy!" neither fear their fear, nor be in dread of it.” (8:12, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… conspiracy!" concerning all about which this people shall say, "A conspiracy!" neither fear their fear, nor be in dread of it.”
Q5. True or false: Isaiah was told to name his son Maher-shalal-hash-baz. (8:1-3) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 8:1 records: “Yahweh said to me, "Take a large tablet, and write on it with a man's pen, 'For Maher Shalal Hash Baz;'”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… an uproar, O you peoples, and be broken in pieces! And give ______, all you of far countries: gird yourselves, and be broken in pieces! Gird …”? (8:9, WEB) Medium
A) ear B) arm C) foot D) hands
Answer: A. ear. The passage specifies ear: “… an uproar, O you peoples, and be broken in pieces! And give ear, all you of far countries: gird yourselves, and be broken in pieces! Gird …”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… for wonders in Israel from Yahweh of Hosts, who dwells in Mount ______.” (8:18); “When they shall tell you, "______ with those who have familiar spirits and with the wizards, who chirp and who mutter:" shouldn't a people consult with …” (8:19, WEB) Hard
Answer: Zion; Consult. Q6. A) water; joy B) figs; joy C) joy; water D) water; kindness
Answer: A. water; joy. Verse 9 reads “… with the weeping of Jazer for the vine of Sibmah; I will water you with my tears, Heshbon, and Elealeh: for on your summer fruits and …”; verse 10 reads “Gladness is taken away, and joy out of the fruitful field; and in the vineyards there shall be no singing, neither joyful noise: nobody shall tread …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q7. True or false: Moab was told its boasting would be brought low. (16:6) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 16:6 records: “We have heard of the pride of Moab, that he is very proud; even of his arrogance, and his pride, and his wrath; his boastings are nothing.”

Answer: D. justice. Isaiah 16:3 uses justice: “Give counsel, execute justice; make your shade as the night in the midst of the noonday; hide the outcasts; don't betray the fugitive.”

But I said, I pine away, I pine away, woe is …”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.

Q6. True or false: Isaiah said a king will reign in righteousness. (32:1) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 32:1 records: “Behold, a king shall reign in righteousness, and princes shall rule in justice.”

Answer: A. spread; Babylon. He whom Yahweh loves shall perform his pleasure on Babylon, and his arm shall be on the Chaldeans.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. True or false: The LORD says He refines His people in the furnace of affliction. (48:10) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 48:10 records: “Behold, I have refined you, but not as silver; I have chosen you in the furnace of affliction.”

Answer: D. brass. Isaiah 48:4 uses brass: “… are obstinate, and your neck is an iron sinew, and your brow brass;”

Answer: False. The actual wording is “… I do it; for how should my name be profaned? and my glory I will not give to another.” It says glory, not faithfulness.

Isaiah 53
Q1. Multiple choice: Why do Christians connect this servant passage with Jesus? (53:4-12; Acts 8:30-35) Hard
A) The chapter names Pontius Pilate. B) It depicts innocent suffering for others, and the New Testament explicitly applies the passage to Jesus. C) It describes the servant as suffering only for his own violence. D) The New Testament avoids the passage entirely.
Answer: B. It depicts innocent suffering for others, and the New Testament explicitly applies the passage to Jesus.. Acts records Philip beginning with this passage to explain the good news of Jesus. The Christian reading should acknowledge that the servant's identity is also debated in interpretation of Isaiah itself.
Q2. Fill in the blank: In this paraphrase, he was despised and rejected by men, a man of sorrows and acquainted with ______. (53:3) Easy
Answer: grief. Isaiah 53:3 records: “He was despised, and rejected by men; a man of suffering, and acquainted with disease: and as one from whom men hide their face he was despised; and we didn't respect him.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… no form nor comeliness; and when we see him, there is no ______ that we should desire him.” (53:2, WEB) Easy

Verse 8 reads “By oppression and judgment he was taken away; and as for his generation, who among them considered that he was cut off out of the land …”; verse 9 reads “… his grave with the wicked, and with a rich man in his death; although he had done no violence, neither was any deceit in his mouth.”. Answer: True. The actual wording is “… to the slaughter, and as a sheep that before its shearers is mute, so he didn't open his mouth.”

Isaiah 56
Q1. Multiple choice: How does the welcome of foreigners and eunuchs expand the vision of the worshiping community? (56:3-8) Hard
A) Only ancestry matters, and covenant faithfulness is irrelevant. B) The passage commands permanent exclusion of every foreigner. C) God's house is described as having no place for prayer. D) Those who hold fast to God's covenant are promised belonging and joy in His house.
Answer: D. Those who hold fast to God's covenant are promised belonging and joy in His house.. The passage explicitly addresses people who fear exclusion. Its vision of a house of prayer for all peoples must be heard alongside earlier assembly restrictions in their own settings.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Thus says Yahweh, Keep you justice, and do righteousness; for my ______ is near to come, and my righteousness to be revealed.” (56:1, WEB) Easy
Answer: salvation. The cited words are “Thus says Yahweh, Keep you justice, and do righteousness; for my salvation is near to come, and my righteousness to be revealed.”
Q3. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… Yahweh of the eunuchs who ______ my Sabbaths, and choose the things that please me, and hold fast my ______:” (56:4, WEB) Medium
Answer: keep; covenant. The wording is “… Yahweh of the eunuchs who keep my Sabbaths, and choose the things that please me, and hold fast my covenant:” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q4. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… his people; neither let the eunuch say, Behold, I am a dry ______.”? (56:3, WEB) Easy
A) accepted B) never C) tree D) dreaming
Answer: C. tree. Isaiah 56:3 uses tree: “… his people; neither let the eunuch say, Behold, I am a dry tree.”
Q5. True or false: The LORD says His house will be a house of prayer for all peoples. (56:7) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 56:7 records: “… I bring to my holy mountain, and make them joyful in my house of prayer: their burnt offerings and their sacrifices shall be accepted on my altar; for my house shall be called a house of …”
Q6. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… the name of Yahweh, to be his servants, everyone who keeps the ______ from profaning it, and holds fast my covenant;”? (56:6, WEB) Medium
A) Sabbath B) south C) Pentecost D) summer

Q7. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… daughters; I will give them an everlasting name, that shall not be blind off.” (56:5, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… daughters; I will give them an everlasting name, that shall not be cut off.” It says cut, not blind.

Q2. Verse 7 reads “There is none who calls on your name, who stirs up himself to take hold of you; for you have hid your face from us, and …”; verse 9 reads “Don't be angry very sore, Yahweh, neither remember iniquity forever: see, look, we beg you, we are all your people.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q4. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… polluted garment: and we all do fade as a leaf; and our iniquities, like the wind, take us away.” (64:6, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “… polluted garment: and we all do fade as a leaf; and our iniquities, like the wind, take us away.”
Q5. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… terrible things which we didn't look for, you came down, the mountains ______ at your presence.”? (64:3, WEB) Easy
A) perceived B) saved C) quaked D) consumed
Answer: C. quaked. Isaiah 64:3 uses quaked: “… terrible things which we didn't look for, you came down, the mountains quaked at your presence.”
Q6. True or false: Isaiah prayed that God would tear open the heavens and come down. (64:1) Easy
Answer: True. Isaiah 64:1 records: “Oh that you would tear the heavens, that you would come down, that the mountains might quake at your presence,”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… men have not heard, nor perceived by the ______, neither has the eye seen a God ______ you, who works for him who waits for him.” (64:4, WEB) Medium
Answer: ear; besides. The wording is “… men have not heard, nor perceived by the ear, neither has the eye seen a God besides you, who works for him who waits for him.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q8. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “as when ______ kindles the brushwood, and the fire causes the waters to boil; to make your name known to your adversaries, that the nations may …” (64:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: fire. The cited words are “as when fire kindles the brushwood, and the fire causes the waters to boil; to make your name known to your adversaries, that the nations may …”

Jeremiah
Jeremiah 1
Q1. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “to whom the word of Yahweh came in the days of ______ the son of Amon, king of Judah, in the thirteenth year of his reign.” (1:2, WEB) Easy
Answer: Josiah. The cited words are “to whom the word of Yahweh came in the days of Josiah the son of Amon, king of Judah, in the thirteenth year of his reign.”
Q2. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… my judgments against them touching all their wickedness, in that they have ______ me, and have burned incense to other gods, and worshiped the works of …” (1:16); “… against the kings of Judah, against the princes of it, against the ______ of it, and against the people of the land.” (1:18, WEB) Hard
Answer: forsaken; priests. The first passage reads “… my judgments against them touching all their wickedness, in that they have forsaken me, and have burned incense to other gods, and worshiped the works of …”; the second reads “… against the kings of Judah, against the princes of it, against the priests of it, and against the people of the land.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q3. Multiple choice: Which word completes “… the son of Josiah, king of Judah, to the carrying away of ______ captive in the fifth month.”? (1:3, WEB) Easy
A) Sinai B) Damascus C) Jerusalem D) Bethel
Answer: C. Jerusalem. Jeremiah 1:3 uses Jerusalem: “… the son of Josiah, king of Judah, to the carrying away of Jerusalem captive in the fifth month.”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… me, Don't say, I am a ______; for to whoever I shall send you, you shall go, and ______ I shall command you, you shall speak.” (1:7, WEB) Medium
Answer: child; whatever. The wording is “… me, Don't say, I am a child; for to whoever I shall send you, you shall go, and whatever I shall command you, you shall speak.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “behold, I have this day set you over the ______ and over the kingdoms, to pluck up and to break down and to destroy and to …”? (1:10, WEB) Medium
A) works B) days C) nations D) gods
Answer: C. nations. The passage specifies nations: “behold, I have this day set you over the nations and over the kingdoms, to pluck up and to break down and to destroy and to …”
Q6. True or false: God knew Jeremiah and appointed him a prophet before he was born. (1:5) Easy
Answer: True. Jeremiah 1:5 records: “Before I formed you in the belly I knew you, and before you came forth out of the womb I sanctified you; I have appointed you a prophet to the nations.”
Q7. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “The word of Yahweh came to me the ______ time, saying, What see you? I said, I see a boiling caldron; and the face of it …” (1:13); “… the walls of it round about, and against all the cities of ______.” (1:15, WEB) Hard
A) five; Judah B) Judah; second C) second; Enoch D) second; Judah
Answer: D. second; Judah. Verse 13 reads “The word of Yahweh came to me the second time, saying, What see you? I said, I see a boiling caldron; and the face of it …”; verse 15 reads “… the walls of it round about, and against all the cities of Judah.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “Moreover the word of Yahweh came to me, saying, Phinehas, what see you? I said, I see a rod of an almond tree.” (1:11, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “Moreover the word of Yahweh came to me, saying, Jeremiah, what see you? I said, I see a rod of an almond tree.” It says Jeremiah, not Phinehas.

Q2. Fill in the blank: Connect the two passages by supplying both missing terms: “… trouble: for according to the number of your cities are your gods, ______.” (2:28); “Can a virgin forget her ornaments, or a ______ her attire? yet my people have forgotten me days without number.” (2:32, WEB) Hard
Answer: Judah; bride. The first passage reads “… trouble: for according to the number of your cities are your gods, Judah.”; the second reads “Can a virgin forget her ornaments, or a bride her attire? yet my people have forgotten me days without number.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q3. Fill in the blank: In this paraphrase, the LORD said the people had forsaken Him, the fountain of living ______. (2:13) Easy
Answer: waters. Jeremiah 2:13 records: “For my people have committed two evils: they have forsaken me, the spring of living waters, and hewed them out cisterns, broken cisterns, that can hold no water.”
Q4. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “Yet I had ______ you a noble vine, wholly a right seed: how then are you turned into the degenerate branches of a foreign vine to …” (2:21); “… but you said, It is in vain; no, for I have loved ______, and after them will I go.” (2:25, WEB) Hard
A) planted; strangers B) changed; strangers C) planted; deserts D) strangers; planted
Answer: A. planted; strangers. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q5. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “Is Israel a ______? is he a home-born slave? why is he become a prey?”? (2:14, WEB) Medium
A) judges B) ruler C) judge D) servant
Answer: D. servant. The passage specifies servant: “Is Israel a servant? is he a home-born slave? why is he become a prey?”
Q6. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Israel was ______ to Yahweh, the first fruits of his increase: all who devour him shall be held guilty; evil shall come on them, says Yahweh.” (2:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: holiness. The cited words are “Israel was holiness to Yahweh, the first fruits of his increase: all who devour him shall be held guilty; evil shall come on them, says Yahweh.”
Q7. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Has a ______ changed its gods, which yet are no gods? but my people have changed their ______ for that which does not profit.” (2:11, WEB) Medium
Answer: nation; glory. The wording is “Has a nation changed its gods, which yet are no gods? but my people have changed their glory for that which does not profit.” The missing terms belong in that order.

Answer: False. The actual wording is “… the Shihor? or what have you to do in the way to Assyria, to drink the waters of the River?” It says Assyria, not Israel.

These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q3. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “Were they ashamed when they had wicked abomination? nay, they were not at all ashamed, neither could they blush: therefore they shall fall among those who …” (6:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “Were they ashamed when they had committed abomination? nay, they were not at all ashamed, neither could they blush: therefore they shall fall among those who …” It says committed, not wicked.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… They shall thoroughly glean the remnant of ______ as a vine: turn again your hand as a ______-gatherer into the baskets.” (6:9, WEB) Medium

Jeremiah 7
Q1. True or false: Jeremiah warned people not to trust in the words, "This is the temple of the LORD." (7:4) Easy
Answer: True. Jeremiah 7:4 records: “Don't you trust in lying words, saying, The temple of Yahweh, the temple of Yahweh, the temple of Yahweh, are these.”
Q2. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “But go you now to my place which was in ______, where I caused my name to dwell at the first, and see what I did …”? (7:12, WEB) Medium
A) Nineveh B) Shiloh C) Sinai D) Jordan
Answer: B. Shiloh. The passage specifies Shiloh: “But go you now to my place which was in Shiloh, where I caused my name to dwell at the first, and see what I did …”
Q3. Multiple choice: Why does repeating temple language fail to guarantee safety? (7:1-15) Hard
A) The temple was never associated with worship. B) Jeremiah promises safety without repentance. C) The people appeal to the temple while persisting in injustice and idolatry. D) God says theft and oppression no longer matter.
Answer: C. The people appeal to the temple while persisting in injustice and idolatry.. Jeremiah calls for changed conduct toward neighbors and the vulnerable. Sacred buildings and religious slogans cannot substitute for covenant faithfulness.
Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Will you steal, ______, and commit adultery, and swear falsely, and burn incense to Baal, and ______ after other gods that you have not known,” (7:9, WEB) Medium

Q5. Multiple choice: Which pair completes BOTH passages in order? “… says the Lord Yahweh: Behold, my anger and my wrath shall be ______ out on this place, on man, and on animal, and on the trees …” (7:20); “… walked in their own counsels and in the stubbornness of their evil ______, and went backward, and not forward.” (7:24, WEB) Hard
A) received; heart B) poured; ear C) heart; poured D) poured; heart
Answer: D. poured; heart. Verse 20 reads “… says the Lord Yahweh: Behold, my anger and my wrath shall be poured out on this place, on man, and on animal, and on the trees …”; verse 24 reads “… walked in their own counsels and in the stubbornness of their evil heart, and went backward, and not forward.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.

The first passage reads “Inhabitant of Lebanon, who makes your nest in the cedars, how greatly to be pitied shall you be when pangs come on you, the pain as …”; the second reads “But to the land whereunto their soul longs to return, there shall they not return.”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.
Q2. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “Thus says Yahweh: Execute you justice and ______, and deliver him who is robbed out of the hand of the oppressor: and do no wrong, do …” (22:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: righteousness. The cited words are “Thus says Yahweh: Execute you justice and righteousness, and deliver him who is robbed out of the hand of the oppressor: and do no wrong, do …”

Q4. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “Then they shall answer, Because they forsook the ______ of Yahweh their God, and ______ other gods, and served them.” (22:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: covenant; worshiped. The wording is “Then they shall answer, Because they forsook the covenant of Yahweh their God, and worshiped other gods, and served them.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q5. Fill in the blank: In this paraphrase, the LORD said Jehoiakim would be buried with the burial of a ______. (22:18-19) Easy
Answer: donkey. Jeremiah 22:19 records: “He shall be buried with the burial of a donkey, drawn and cast forth beyond the gates of Jerusalem.”
Q6. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… to excel in cedar? Didn't your father eat and drink, and do glory and righteousness? then it was well with him.” (22:15, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… to excel in cedar? Didn't your father eat and drink, and do justice and righteousness? then it was well with him.” It says justice, not glory.
Q7. Jeremiah 29
Q1. Multiple choice: Which detail belongs in this passage: “… turn again your captivity, and I will gather you from all the ______, and from all the places where I have driven you, says Yahweh; and …”? (29:14, WEB) Medium
A) diviners B) nations C) eunuchs D) princes
Answer: B. nations. The passage specifies nations: “… turn again your captivity, and I will gather you from all the nations, and from all the places where I have driven you, says Yahweh; and …”
Q2. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “thus says Yahweh concerning the king who sits on the throne of David, and concerning all the people who dwell in this city, your brothers who …” (29:16, WEB) Medium
Answer: True. The actual wording is “thus says Yahweh concerning the king who sits on the throne of David, and concerning all the people who dwell in this city, your brothers who …”
Q3. Multiple choice: Which word completes “Take ______, and father sons and daughters; and take wives for your sons, and give your daughters to husbands, that they may bear sons and daughters; …”? (29:6, WEB) Easy
A) wives B) husbands C) prophets D) judge
Answer: A. wives. Jeremiah 29:6 uses wives: “Take wives, and father sons and daughters; and take wives for your sons, and give your daughters to husbands, that they may bear sons and daughters; …”
Q4. Fill in the blank: Complete the quotation: “… Gemariah the son of Hilkiah, (whom Zedekiah king of Judah sent to ______ to Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon), saying,” (29:3, WEB) Easy
Answer: Babylon. The cited words are “… Gemariah the son of Hilkiah, (whom Zedekiah king of Judah sent to Babylon to Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon), saying,”
Q5. Multiple choice: What does the promise of a future and hope mean in its immediate setting? (29:4-14) Hard
A) Every individual plan is guaranteed to succeed immediately. B) The exiles will return the next morning. C) The exiles are forbidden to pray for Babylon. D) It addresses exiles facing a long stay in Babylon and promised restoration.
Answer: D. It addresses exiles facing a long stay in Babylon and promised restoration.. The letter calls for settled, faithful life and prayer for the city, and names seventy years. It is not a guarantee that every reader's immediate plans will succeed.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… says Yahweh, After ______ years are accomplished for Babylon, I will visit you, and perform my good word toward you, in ______ you to return to …” (29:10, WEB) Medium
Answer: seventy; causing. The wording is “… says Yahweh, After seventy years are accomplished for Babylon, I will visit you, and perform my good word toward you, in causing you to return to …” The missing terms belong in that order.

Answer: priest; build. The first passage reads “… who are at Jerusalem, and to Zephaniah the son of Maaseiah, the priest, and to all the priests, saying,”; the second reads “… he has sent to us in Babylon, saying, The captivity is long: build you houses, and dwell in them; and plant gardens, and eat the fruit …”. Keeping the details with their own passages gives the stated order.

Q2. Multiple choice: What characterizes the promised new covenant? (31:31-34) Hard
A) Only a larger stone tablet will change. B) God's law written on hearts, knowledge of God and forgiveness. C) God will stop caring about faithfulness. D) No one will need forgiveness.
Answer: B. God's law written on hearts, knowledge of God and forgiveness.. The promise responds to a broken covenant relationship. Luke 22:20 and Hebrews 8 draw on it in Christian teaching about Jesus and the new covenant.

A) Solomon; six B) Leah; six C) Solomon; eight D) six; Solomon
Answer: A. Solomon; six. Verse 20 reads “… and the twelve brazen bulls that were under the bases, which king Solomon had made for the house of Yahweh. The brass of all these vessels …”; verse 23 reads “There were ninety-six pomegranates on the sides; all the pomegranates were one hundred on the network round about.”. These establish the first and second answers respectively.
Q6. Fill in the blank: Supply the missing words in order: “… and carried him up to the ______ of Babylon to Riblah in the land of Hamath; and he gave ______ on him.” (52:9, WEB) Medium
Answer: king; judgment. The wording is “… and carried him up to the king of Babylon to Riblah in the land of Hamath; and he gave judgment on him.” The missing terms belong in that order.
Q7. Multiple choice: Which word completes “In the fourth month, in the ______ day of the month, the famine was sore in the city, so that there was no bread for the …”? (52:6, WEB) Easy
A) second B) ninth C) seventeen D) eleven
Answer: B. ninth. Jeremiah 52:6 uses ninth: “In the fourth month, in the ninth day of the month, the famine was sore in the city, so that there was no bread for the …”
Q8. True or false: The following quotation matches the cited verse: “… of Yahweh, did the Chaldeans break in pieces, and carried all the wood of them to Babylon.” (52:17, WEB) Medium
Answer: False. The actual wording is “… of Yahweh, did the Chaldeans break in pieces, and carried all the brass of them to Babylon.” It says brass, not wood.
